I've read through the logs. The app is caught in an infinite reconnect loop on your Mac:
1. Invalid symbols — Fyers rejects hundreds of delisted/suspended NSE stocks (AAKAAR-EQ, ABAN-EQ, GENSOL-EQ, etc.) with error code -300.
2. The strip isn't persisted — After each rejection the code strips those symbols in memory and reconnects, but the next reconnect rebuilds the subscription list from the full universe, re-adding the same bad symbols. So it loops endlessly.
3. Side effect — The reconnect storm is burning your Fyers API quota (request limit reached).

Now I have a clear picture. Let me also quickly check what keeps calling subscribeSymbols during the loop.



3 actions
Here's the precise diagnosis, with exact code locations:

What needs to be fixed
There is one root cause driving everything you see in the logs.
Root cause: No invalid-symbol blacklist
When Fyers rejects a symbol with code -300, liveFeedService.ts lines 671–673 do this:
subscriptions.remove(invalidSymbols); // ✓ removes from active set
// ✗ nothing prevents re-adding them

The symbols are removed from SubscriptionManager, but there's no blacklist. Within milliseconds, marketDataService.ts line 835 calls subscribeSymbols(missing) again — it reads from your symbols table (which has ~2923 rows, many of which are delisted on Fyers) and tries to subscribe anything not currently tracked. The just-stripped invalid symbols re-enter the 200-slot window. Next reconnect, Fyers rejects them again. Loop forever.

The three symptoms that fall out from this
Log line	What's really happening
Stripping N invalid symbols…	Good — code detects them
Reconnecting in 1000ms (attempt 1)	Starts fresh, but no blacklist
Evicting N oldest unprotected symbols	subscribeSymbols() called between reconnects, cycling the full 2923-symbol universe through the 200-slot cap
Fyers quotes request failed: request limit reached	Side effect — reconnect storm burns REST API quota too
What to fix
1 — Add an in-memory invalid-symbol blacklist in liveFeedService.ts
A module-level Set<string> populated when Fyers reports bad symbols, then filtered in subscribeSymbols() before anything reaches the SubscriptionManager. Invalid symbols can never re-enter the subscription window for the rest of the session.
2 — Optionally persist to DB (stronger fix)
Write the blacklisted symbols to a fyers_invalid_symbols table (or a flag column on symbols) so they're excluded even across app restarts. Without this, the storm happens again every cold start until all invalids are learned again in-session.
3 — The underlying data problem
The symbols table contains ~2923 stocks but Fyers only recognises a subset of them — many are suspended, delisted, or renamed. The blacklist fix stops the loop, but the cleaner long-term fix is to purge or flag these dead symbols in the DB so they never attempt to subscribe in the first place.

Want me to implement the fix?

dont do anything right now, this is what claude AI is saying about you fixes, please read tell me what do you think ? "Replit's diagnosis of the loop mechanism is correct — I can see it clearly in your logs (same batches of symbols: AAKAAR/AARADHYA/AAREYDRUGS... cycling every reconnect, evicting 40-50 symbols each time, hitting request limit reached from the reconnect storm). The blacklist fix is real and worth doing. But it's treating the symptom, not the disease — and I think there's a more specific root cause underneath that Replit's writeup glosses over.

The real question: why are these specific symbols still being built as -EQ?

Your memory shows Prompt 31/32 already fixed toFyersSymbol() to read the fyers_symbol column from Fyers' own CM master CSV instead of hardcoding NSE:{symbol}-EQ — and you confirmed it working (NSE:AAKAAR-SM resolving correctly) after running /api/symbols/refresh.

But these logs — from the new laptop, first run — show NSE:AAKAAR-EQ being rejected, not NSE:AAKAAR-SM. Same for DIVINEHIRA, ARISTO, KOPRAN, and hundreds of others in the same batches that were fixed on your other machine.

That tells me one of two things is happening, and they need different fixes:

This new laptop's market.db has never had /api/symbols/refresh run, so the fyers_symbol column is null/empty for these rows, and whatever code builds the live-feed subscription list is falling back to the old -EQ guess. This is a data/setup issue, not a code bug — you just need to run the refresh on this machine.
The live feed's subscription-building path (liveFeedService.ts / marketDataService.ts's subscribeSymbols) never uses toFyersSymbol()/fyers_symbol at all — it might have its own separate NSE:{symbol}-EQ construction that Prompt 31 never touched. In that case the fix only ever propagated to the sync/EOD call sites, and the live feed subscription list has been broken (or half-broken) this whole time on both machines, and you didn't notice until now because the sync jobs (which did get the fix) don't hit this path.

I'd bet on #2 being at least partially true, because Prompt 31/32's description in your memory specifically mentions fixing symbol resolution for EOD sync and optionsDataService.ts — nothing about liveFeedService.ts's own symbol construction for live subscriptions. If the live feed subscribes using raw symbols table data and appends -EQ itself rather than reading the already-fixed fyers_symbol column, that would exactly explain this.

What I'd want to see before approving either fix
The actual code in liveFeedService.ts (or wherever it builds NSE:XXX-EQ for the F&O auto-subscribe list) — specifically, does it query fyers_symbol from the symbols table, or does it construct the ticker itself?
A quick DB check on this new laptop: SELECT symbol, fyers_symbol FROM symbols WHERE symbol IN ('AAKAAR','DIVINEHIRA','ARISTO') LIMIT 5; — if fyers_symbol is null/empty here, it's a refresh-not-run issue (fix: run /api/symbols/refresh on this machine); if it's populated correctly but still failing, it's a code path that ignores the column.
On Replit's blacklist proposal itself

It's a good defensive addition regardless of the above — even with symbol resolution perfect, Fyers will occasionally suspend/delist something mid-session, and a blacklist (in-memory + persisted to a fyers_invalid_symbols table or is_delisted-style flag) stops the reconnect storm from burning your quota. I'd approve prompts 1 and 2 from their proposal as written. But I'd hold off on treating this as "the fix" until you've confirmed whether it's masking the symbol-resolution regression above — otherwise you'll silently blacklist hundreds of perfectly valid, currently-listed stocks just because the live feed is guessing their suffix wrong, and your F&O auto-subscribe pool will quietly shrink over time.

The smoking gun
typescript
function toFyersSymbol(symbol: string): string {
  return symbol.includes(":") ? symbol : `NSE:${symbol}-EQ`;
}

This is a second, independent toFyersSymbol() living entirely inside liveFeedService.ts. It has nothing to do with the toFyersSymbol() that Prompt 31 fixed (which presumably lives in marketDataService.ts or a shared symbol-resolution module and correctly reads the fyers_symbol column). This one never got touched. It still hardcodes NSE:{symbol}-EQ for every non-prefixed symbol, exactly like the pre-Prompt-31 bug.

And it's not a dead code path — it's actively used by the highest-volume subscriber:

typescript
function rankFoSymbolsByPrice(): FoRankedSymbol[] {
  return marketDb.prepare(`SELECT s.symbol AS symbol, ... FROM symbols s ...`)...
}

export function autoSubscribeFoSymbols(...) {
  const ranked = rankFoSymbolsByPrice();          // bare symbols, e.g. "AAKAAR"
  const chosen = ranked.slice(0, limit).map(r => r.symbol);
  const result = subscribeSymbols(chosen, { protect: true });  // → toFyersSymbol() → "NSE:AAKAAR-EQ" ❌
}

rankFoSymbolsByPrice() pulls the bare symbol column (not fyers_symbol), and subscribeSymbols() runs every one of those through the local naive toFyersSymbol(). So every SME/BE/ST-series stock in your F&O-eligible, lowest-priced universe gets built as -EQ and rejected by Fyers — precisely the AAKAAR, DIVINEHIRA, ARISTO, KOPRAN pattern you're seeing, batch after batch, forever, because the eviction/reconnect cycle keeps re-selecting the same cheap stocks from the same ranked list.

Why this wasn't caught before

Prompt 31/32 fixed symbol resolution for EOD sync and options sync — both of which likely go through marketDataService.ts's adapter layer. liveFeedService.ts was explicitly built as a separate, protocol-isolated module (per its own header comment) and nobody re-ran the symbol-fix audit against it. It has its own local copy of the same naive logic the other fix replaced.

The correct fix (in order)
Make liveFeedService.ts use the real, fixed symbol resolution instead of its own toFyersSymbol(). Either import the shared fixed version, or change rankFoSymbolsByPrice()'s query to SELECT s.fyers_symbol AS symbol (falling back to the old logic only if fyers_symbol is null) and drop the local naive function entirely.
Add the invalid-symbol blacklist Replit proposed anyway — as defense-in-depth for symbols that are correctly resolved but later get delisted/suspended mid-session. It's a legitimate second layer, just not the primary fix.
Skip persisting the blacklist to a new DB table for now — you already have is_delisted derived from CSV presence (Prompt 32); an in-memory Set cleared on restart is sufficient once #1 stops the flood at the source.

5 actions
Now I have the complete answer. The symbols are all from Fyers — there's no mystery second source.
Here's exactly what happens:
The symbol universe comes from Fyers' own public CSV:
https://public.fyers.in/sym_details/NSE_CM.csv

This file lists every security on the NSE Capital Market segment. The app downloads it, parses it, and stores ~2981 rows. Column 9 of that CSV is Fyers' own ticker string — things like NSE:AAKAAR-SM, NSE:DIVINEHIRA-BE, NSE:ARISTO-SM — and the code correctly saves this into the fyers_symbol column in the DB (line 252 of symbolMasterService.ts).
The bug is that the live feed ignores what it already knows:
The DB has the correct Fyers symbol. But liveFeedService.ts throws it away and guesses:
function toFyersSymbol(symbol: string): string {
return symbol.includes(":") ? symbol : `NSE:${symbol}-EQ`; // always -EQ
}

So AAKAAR (which the DB knows is NSE:AAKAAR-SM) gets sent to the WebSocket as NSE:AAKAAR-EQ. Fyers rejects it — not because the stock doesn't exist on Fyers, but because the suffix is wrong.
In short:
Stock	What's in DB (fyers_symbol)	What live feed sends	Fyers' reaction
AAKAAR	NSE:AAKAAR-SM (SME segment)	NSE:AAKAAR-EQ	❌ Invalid
DIVINEHIRA	NSE:DIVINEHIRA-BE (trade-for-trade)	NSE:DIVINEHIRA-EQ	❌ Invalid
RELIANCE	NSE:RELIANCE-EQ	NSE:RELIANCE-EQ	✅ Valid
The stock exists in Fyers. The data is there. The live feed just constructs the wrong address for it. The fix is simply: use fyers_symbol from the DB instead of hardcoding -EQ.


this is claude's reply "That confirms it exactly — and it's the same conclusion I reached from the code, just verified against the actual CSV source now. Good sanity-check to run.

So to directly answer your question: your app does have only one source (Fyers' NSE_CM.csv), and every one of these symbols genuinely exists in that file. Nothing is being invented or pulled from elsewhere. The "invalid symbol" isn't Fyers saying "I don't have this stock" — it's Fyers saying "the string you sent me doesn't match any stock I have," because -EQ isn't the right suffix for SME/BE-series names. The data was sitting right there in fyers_symbol (already fetched, already stored, already correct) and liveFeedService.ts just never looked at it.

The fix, concretely

Two places need to change in liveFeedService.ts:

1. rankFoSymbolsByPrice() — currently selects the bare s.symbol. Change to prefer fyers_symbol:

sql
SELECT s.symbol AS symbol,
       COALESCE(s.fyers_symbol, s.symbol) AS resolved_symbol,
       latest.close AS price
  FROM symbols s
  JOIN (...) latest ON latest.symbol = s.symbol
 WHERE s.is_fo_eligible = 1
   AND latest.close IS NOT NULL
 ORDER BY latest.close ASC

Then use resolved_symbol (not symbol) as what gets passed into subscribeSymbols().

2. toFyersSymbol() — right now it's a dumb fallback that assumes -EQ. Once callers are passing the already-correct fyers_symbol (which already contains NSE:...-SM, NSE:...-BE, etc. — i.e. already contains :), this function's includes(":") branch will just pass it through unchanged, which is correct. The only remaining risk is other call sites that still pass bare tickers without going through fyers_symbol (e.g. any ad-hoc chart-view subscribe from the frontend) — worth a quick grep for other callers of subscribeSymbols( to confirm none of them are constructing bare symbols independently.

3. Keep the blacklist as defense-in-depth — even with this fixed, Fyers can still suspend a stock mid-session (corporate action, trading halt classification change) between your weekly symbol-master refresh and now. The blacklist stops that from causing a reconnect storm; it's just no longer the primary fix.

One thing worth checking before you ship this: confirm fyers_symbol is actually populated (non-null) for all is_fo_eligible = 1 rows on this laptop's DB — if /api/symbols/refresh hasn't been run here yet, the COALESCE will silently fall back to the bare symbol and you'll get the exact same failure, just harder to notice since it won't error at the query level."

Claude's fix is correct and I agree with all three points. A few thoughts:
On the two code changes — both are right. The COALESCE(s.fyers_symbol, s.symbol) query change is the cleanest approach. And Claude is correct that once callers pass an already-prefixed fyers_symbol (which contains :), toFyersSymbol() just passes it through unchanged via the existing includes(":") branch — no rewrite needed there.
On the grep suggestion — this is important and I should have flagged it earlier. subscribeSymbols() has at least three other callers besides autoSubscribeFoSymbols:
* marketDataService.ts:835 — subscribes symbols when serving quote requests
* liveOptionsTracker.ts:218, 324, 393 — subscribes option spot symbols and contracts
* marketData.ts route:188 — ad-hoc subscribe from the frontend (chart views)
The options tracker is probably fine (it constructs Fyers-format symbols like NSE:NIFTY50-INDEX itself). The frontend route is the one to watch — if the UI sends bare tickers like "RELIANCE" it'll still hit the -EQ fallback, which for main-board stocks is actually correct. So that one is low risk.
On the pre-ship DB check — this is the most important thing Claude said. If fyers_symbol is NULL for any is_fo_eligible = 1 row on the new laptop (because symbol refresh hasn't run), the COALESCE silently falls back to bare symbol → same failure, harder to debug. Worth running the query before shipping.
In summary: Claude's fix plan is complete and correct. Ready to implement whenever you say go — it's a small, targeted change.
